# Digest 20261002-100614

Date: 2026-10-02. **Backfill run** requested by the user (`since: 2026-01-01`), the first with the fixed `monitor.py` (commit e5c5adf): long PubMed windows are now split by date instead of being cut at 500, and feeds are parsed without `feedparser`. Searches run: egfr-nsclc, nsclc-resistance-nextgen, lung-key-evidence, lung-top-journals, lung-nonpharm, preprints. approvals-access was left out because its web queries already ran in run 20261002-093031 an hour earlier.

## Source errors and gaps

- **No source errors.** The medRxiv and bioRxiv feeds worked for the first time today (they had failed in every earlier run today because `feedparser` could not be installed).
- **No truncation.** This run closes the gap left by runs 20261002-072943 and 20261002-093031, which retrieved only 500 of the matches per search for the same window. The PubMed windows were split into 2-7 date slices (`slices` in `state/runs.jsonl`), and every match was retrieved.
- The preprint feeds only list the latest 30 posts, so they cannot be backfilled; preprints from earlier in 2026 that were missed while the feeds failed are not recoverable this way.
- Because this is a backfill, items date from January-September 2026, and many report trials that have already been widely discussed. Triage was done in parallel batches from titles, publication types and abstracts; 104 items had no abstract and were judged from the title.

## High

### egfr-nsclc

- **Patient-Reported Outcomes in FLAURA2: Osimertinib with or without Chemotherapy in Patients with Previously Untreated EGFR-Mutated Advanced Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer.**: This is the patient-reported outcome analysis of the phase III FLAURA2 trial (first-line osimertinib with or without platinum-pemetrexed; PFS HR 0.62). It used EORTC QLQ-C30/LC13 and PRO-CTCAE. Changes in global health status/QoL and physical function were small and not clinically meaningful in either arm (LSM change 3.32 with the combination vs 7.38 with monotherapy). Cough improved meaningfully in both arms, and fatigue and appetite loss worsened slightly, below the clinically meaningful threshold, during combination induction. This supports the tolerability of the combination from the patient's perspective. Limitations: the trial was open-label, patients had only mild symptoms at baseline, and the abstract gives no patient numbers.
  Gray JE, Laktionov K, Kim SW et al. *Clin Cancer Res*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41801128/
- **Genomic Profiling of Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor Mutation-Positive Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer after Progression on First-line Osimertinib: Phase II ORCHARD Study.**: This is a prespecified exploratory genomic analysis of the phase II biomarker-directed ORCHARD study, covering tissue (n=400) and plasma (n=191) after progression on first-line osimertinib. Tissue and plasma NGS together found resistance alterations in 87% of samples, and 46% had more than one. PI3K-pathway, SOX2 and MYC alterations were common in histologically transformed tumours, and co-occurring EGFR mutations differed between L858R and exon 19 deletion. This heterogeneity supports combination approaches after osimertinib. Limitation: the analysis is exploratory and descriptive, with no outcome data linked to specific mechanisms in the abstract.
  Yu HA, Tang KH, Markovets A et al. *Clin Cancer Res*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41790029/
- **Upfront treatment with osimertinib in lung cancer patients with and without active brain metastases, and the role of ctDNA as a biomarker; a phase II clinical trial (the FIOL study).**: The single-arm phase II FIOL trial (n=100; 46 with active, untreated brain metastases) gave first-line osimertinib to patients with EGFR-mutated NSCLC. ORR was 72.0% overall (69.6% vs 74.1% with vs without brain metastases), and intracranial ORR was 81.8% in measurable brain metastases. PFS and OS did not differ between the cohorts. L858R/uncommon mutations and detectable baseline ctDNA (85.6% of samples) were each associated with shorter PFS and OS. This is prospective evidence on CNS efficacy and on ctDNA as a prognostic marker. Limitations: no randomised comparator, and the analyses by ctDNA and mutation subtype are exploratory.
  Stensland EM, Stensgaard S, Eide IJZ et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41723914/
- **2025 Taiwan guidelines on the drug therapy of lung cancer: Advanced non-squamous cell carcinoma with actionable oncogenic drivers.**: Six Taiwanese medical societies jointly issued this national guideline on first- and second-line drug therapy for advanced non-squamous NSCLC with actionable drivers, including EGFR. Recommendations are graded by level of evidence (LoE I, large RCTs, to LoE V, expert opinion). It is a regional guideline update relevant to EGFR treatment sequencing. Limitation: the abstract does not list the specific EGFR recommendations, and the guideline is tailored to Taiwanese practice and reimbursement.
  Wu SG, Ho CC, Taiwan Society of Pulmonary and Critical Care Medicine et al. *J Formos Med Assoc*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41708470/
- **Overall survival for amivantamab plus lazertinib versus osimertinib as first-line treatment in Asian participants with EGFR-mutant advanced NSCLC: A MARIPOSA subset analysis.**: This analysis covers the 629 self-identified Asian participants in the phase III MARIPOSA trial (Asian race was a stratification factor). At a median follow-up of 38.7 months, amivantamab-lazertinib prolonged OS compared with osimertinib: median not reached vs 38.4 months, HR 0.74 (95% CI 0.56-0.97), nominal P=0.026, and 36-month OS of 61% vs 53%. Safety matched the overall population. The result is consistent with the overall OS benefit (HR 0.75). Limitations: this is a subgroup analysis with a nominal p-value, and the >12-month median OS gain is a model-based projection, not an observed value.
  Hayashi H, Cho BC, Kim YJ et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41689889/
- **Prevention and management of amivantamab-induced dermatologic toxicities: a European expert consensus and practical algorithm.**: An international panel, including members of the EADV task force on dermatology for cancer patients, reviewed trial and real-world data and reached consensus over several rounds. The panel proposes prevention and monitoring strategies and step-by-step management algorithms for acneiform eruption, EPD-like scalp reactions, paronychia, anogenital ulcerations and mucositis. It also introduces new severity grading for scalp and anogenital lesions. This is practical guidance for an EGFR-MET bispecific now used first line. Limitation: the recommendations are expert opinion and the new grading systems have not been validated.
  Vasiliki N, Vincent S, Zoe A et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41671631/
- **Patient-reported outcomes and time to symptomatic progression from PAPILLON: amivantamab plus chemotherapy vs chemotherapy as first-line treatment of EGFR exon 20 insertion-mutated advanced NSCLC.**: This is the PRO analysis of the open-label phase III PAPILLON trial (n=308 randomised 1:1). At 12 months, 77% of patients on amivantamab-chemotherapy vs 60% on chemotherapy were free of symptomatic progression (HR 0.67, 95% CI 0.46-0.98, p=0.04). Physical functioning and global health status were maintained in both arms. This adds patient-centred support to first-line amivantamab-chemotherapy for exon 20 insertions. Limitation: open-label assessment of PROs and symptomatic progression, and the confidence interval is wide.
  Paz-Ares L, Veillon R, Majem M et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41671629/
- **JIN-A02, a Mutant-Selective Fourth-Generation EGFR Inhibitor, Overcomes C797S-Mediated Resistance and Demonstrates Intracranial Activity in NSCLC.**: This preclinical study tested the fourth-generation EGFR TKI JIN-A02 in C797S/T790M-mutant NSCLC models. It inhibited EGFR signalling more strongly than osimertinib and caused substantial tumour regression in subcutaneous and orthotopic intracranial xenografts, without notable toxicity. Early clinical trial data are mentioned only briefly: three patients had partial responses. It is a candidate for C797S resistance after osimertinib, including CNS disease. Limitations: mostly preclinical, and the clinical signal is anecdotal with no denominator given.
  Lee EJ, Ko JA, Kim MJ et al. *Clin Cancer Res*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41649868/
- **HBE-843, a Novel, Potent, and Selective EGFR Targeting PROTAC for the Treatment of Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer.**: HBE-843 is a CRBN-based EGFR PROTAC that spares wild-type EGFR. It degraded L858R, exon 19 deletion, T790M and C797S mutants (DC50 1.9-18 nM, Dmax >90%; GI50 26-103 nM) and gave 112% tumour growth inhibition in vivo in an L858R-driven model. It is an early degrader candidate aimed at C797S resistance. Limitations: lead-compound stage; the in vivo data come from an L858R model rather than a C797S model, and there are no clinical data.
  Baig MH, Kim CJ, Dattatraya NS et al. *Arch Pharm (Weinheim)*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41635121/
- **First-line Aumolertinib (EGFR tyrosine kinase inhibitor) plus apatinib (VEGFR inhibitor) versus aumolertinib in EGFR-mutant non-small cell lung cancer patients: a randomized, multicenter, phase II trial.**: ATTENTION is an open-label randomised phase II trial at 18 Chinese centres (n=104: 53 combination, 51 monotherapy). At a median follow-up of 19.4 months, aumolertinib plus apatinib improved the 18-month PFS rate (74% vs 50%, P=0.036), median PFS (not reached vs 20.1 months; HR 0.41) and ORR (79% vs 59%). Grade 3 TRAEs were more frequent (38% vs 27%; mainly hypertension and thrombocytopenia), with no grade 4/5 events. This is randomised evidence for an oral TKI-plus-antiangiogenic first-line strategy, with exploratory benefit concentrated in TP53-mutant tumours. Limitations: phase II, open-label, modest size, immature OS, and the comparator was not an osimertinib-based standard.
  Zhang F, Zheng Z, Zhang H et al. *Signal Transduct Target Ther*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41629265/
- **Lazertinib with stereotactic body radiotherapy in oligometastatic EGFR-mutant non-small-cell lung cancer.**: ABLATE is a randomised, non-comparative phase II multicentre trial (n=67) of first-line lazertinib alone (n=34) or with SBRT (n=33) in EGFR-mutant synchronous oligometastatic NSCLC. Median PFS was 34.0 months with lazertinib plus SBRT and 24.8 months with lazertinib alone. ORR was 58% and 68%. 92% of SBRT-arm progressions occurred at new sites, and there was no grade >=3 radiation pneumonitis. This supports the feasibility of local consolidative radiotherapy with a 3rd-generation TKI. Limitations: the design is non-comparative, so the arms cannot be formally compared; the trial is small and follow-up is about 23 months.
  Lee JB, Kim KH, Yoon S et al. *ESMO Open*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41604814/
- **Double-dose firmonertinib as first-line treatment in patients with locally advanced or metastatic non-small-cell lung cancer harboring EGFR L858R mutation: a prospective, multicenter, phase II study (FIRM).**: FIRM is a single-arm phase II trial (n=33) of first-line firmonertinib 160 mg/day in L858R-mutant advanced NSCLC. Results: median PFS 21.1 months (primary endpoint), ORR 75.8%, 18-month PFS rate 63.1%, median OS not reached, and grade >=3 TEAEs in 6.1%. Baseline ctDNA VAF predicted ctDNA clearance at cycle 3, which correlated with longer PFS. This is a dose-intensification strategy for the L858R subgroup, which does less well on standard TKIs. Limitations: small, single-arm, no randomised comparison with standard-dose third-generation TKIs.
  Shen B, Wang C, Zhang L et al. *Nat Commun*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41554745/
- **Aumolertinib with carboplatin-pemetrexed versus aumolertinib for nonsmall cell lung cancer with EGFR and concomitant tumor suppressor genes (ACROSS2): An open-label, multicenter, randomized phase 3 study.**: An open-label, multicentre randomised phase 3 trial (n=126) tested first-line aumolertinib plus carboplatin-pemetrexed against aumolertinib alone in EGFR-mutant NSCLC with co-existing tumour suppressor gene mutations. The combination prolonged median PFS (19.8 vs 16.5 months, HR 0.58, 95% CI 0.34-0.97; 24-month PFS 41.0% vs 29.9%), with the clearest benefit in TP53-co-mutated patients. Grade ≥3 adverse events were more frequent with the combination (25.9% vs 17.2%). It is the first prospective test of genotype-directed intensification, but the trial is small, open-label, and OS was immature (4% maturity).
  Duan JC, Zhong J, Sun BY et al. *CA Cancer J Clin*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41818162/
- **Rezivertinib in EGFR-Mutated Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Patients with Central Nervous System Metastasis: Central Nervous System Efficacy from the Phase III REZOR Study.**: This is a CNS analysis of the randomised phase 3 REZOR trial (369 treatment-naive patients; rezivertinib vs gefitinib), covering the 159 patients with baseline CNS metastases on blinded central review. Median CNS PFS was 24.9 vs 15.2 months (HR 0.58, 95% CI 0.34-0.99, p=0.047). CNS ORR in the 25 patients with measurable lesions did not differ significantly (83.3% vs 76.9%). These are CNS efficacy data for another third-generation TKI. The comparator is a first-generation TKI rather than osimertinib, the result is a subgroup analysis with borderline significance, and the response set is very small.
  Yang S, Zhao Y, Sun M et al. *Cancer Commun (Lond)*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41924561/
- **Patient-Relevant Outcomes From the Phase III MARIPOSA-2 Trial: Amivantamab-Chemotherapy Versus Chemotherapy in EGFR-Mutant Advanced Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer Following Disease Progression on Osimertinib.**: This paper reports patient-reported outcomes from the phase 3 MARIPOSA-2 trial after osimertinib progression (amivantamab-chemotherapy n=131 vs chemotherapy n=263). Time to symptomatic progression was longer with amivantamab-chemotherapy (HR 0.73, 95% CI 0.55-0.96, nominal p=0.026). At 6 months, 55.1% vs 29.1% reported improved or stable global health status/QoL, while mean PRO scores showed little difference between arms. This supports the view that the PFS gain after osimertinib does not cost quality of life. The p-values are nominal, and open-label PRO assessment can be biased.
  Tomasini P, Juan-Vidal O, Califano R et al. *Clin Lung Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41544603/
- **Osimertinib with or without savolitinib as first-line treatment for MET-aberrant, EGFR-mutant NSCLC: randomized phase 2 trial (FLOWERS).**: The randomised, open-label phase 2 FLOWERS trial (n=44) gave treatment-naive EGFR-mutant NSCLC with de novo MET amplification or overexpression either osimertinib alone (n=23) or osimertinib plus savolitinib (n=21). Confirmed ORR was 90.5% with the combination vs 60.9% with osimertinib alone. Grade ≥3 treatment-related AEs were much more frequent with the combination (57.1% vs 8.7%). This is randomised evidence for upfront MET co-targeting in de novo MET-aberrant disease. The primary endpoint was response in a very small sample, and the abstract gives no PFS or OS.
  Li A, Feng WN, Li J et al. *Nat Commun*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41530133/
- **Comparative efficacy and safety of post-TKI treatments for advanced EGFR-mutant non-small-cell lung cancer: a systematic review and network meta-analysis.**: This PROSPERO-registered Bayesian network meta-analysis covers 11 phase 3 RCTs (3,650 patients, 7 regimens) after EGFR-TKI failure. Compared with chemotherapy, the following improved both PFS and OS:
  - sacituzumab tirumotecan: PFS HR 0.49, OS HR 0.60
  - amivantamab-chemotherapy: PFS HR 0.48, OS HR 0.73
  - chemo-immunotherapy plus anti-VEGF: PFS HR 0.53, OS HR 0.83
  Sac-TMT also improved OS over chemo-immunotherapy (HR 0.68). Amivantamab-chemotherapy carried more grade ≥3 TRAEs (OR 2.83). This is a useful comparison across post-osimertinib options, but it rests on indirect comparisons of trials with differing populations and prior TKIs, not head-to-head data.
  Wang Y, Zhang L, Zhan J et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41520595/
- **Atezolizumab Combined With Cisplatin Plus Vinorelbine as Adjuvant Therapy for Completely Resected NSCLC With EGFR Mutation (West Japan Oncology Group 11719L: ADJUST Study).**: The single-arm multicentre phase 2 ADJUST trial (WJOG 11719L) gave adjuvant atezolizumab plus cisplatin-vinorelbine, then up to 12 months of atezolizumab, after resection of stage II-IIIA NSCLC with EGFR exon 19 deletion or L858R. The 2-year DFS rate was 44.4%, and median DFS was 21.1 months vs 17.7 months in propensity-matched chemotherapy controls from IMPACT. The authors concluded there was no significant DFS improvement. This is negative evidence for adding checkpoint inhibitors in the adjuvant setting for EGFR-mutant disease. The main limitations are the single-arm design with an external control (sample size not given in the abstract) and the absence of an osimertinib comparator.
  Shibaki R, Akamatsu H, Okada M et al. *JTO Clin Res Rep*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41953249/
- **Long‑term outcomes of prophylactic cranial irradiation in high‑risk metastatic NSCLC: Final PRoT‑BM analysis.**: This is the final analysis of a single-centre, open-label randomised phase 2 trial (84 randomised, 81 analysed) of prophylactic cranial irradiation (25 Gy/10 fractions) vs observation in stage IIIB/IV NSCLC at high risk of brain metastases. Results at a median follow-up of 77 months:
  - 5-year brain-metastasis incidence: 51.7% vs 78.3% (HR 0.40)
  - median OS: 30.5 vs 19.2 months (HR 0.46)
  - EGFR-mutant sensitivity analysis: consistent benefit (HR 0.57, 95% CI 0.33-0.99)
  - MMSE-measured neurocognition: no deterioration
  This bears on CNS prevention strategy, but patients were enrolled in 2012-2017, the trial comes from a single centre, uses only MMSE for cognition, and needs phase 3 confirmation.
  Cabrera-Miranda L, Maldonado F, Rios-Garcia E et al. *Radiother Oncol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41831620/
- **Subcutaneous Delivery of Amivantamab in Patients With Advanced Solid Malignancies: The Phase Ib PALOMA Study.**: Phase Ib dose-escalation study of subcutaneous amivantamab in 158 participants with advanced solid tumours. Safety was largely consistent with IV amivantamab, with fewer and milder infusion-related reactions than historical IV data and administration time of 10 minutes or less (vs 2.3 hours IV beyond cycle 3); RP2Ds were defined for Q2W (1600 mg), Q3W (2400 mg) and Q4W (3520 mg), with higher doses at 80 kg or more. This is the dose-finding basis for subcutaneous delivery of an approved EGFR-MET bispecific in EGFR-mutant NSCLC. Limitation: phase Ib in mixed tumour types; comparisons with IV rely on historical data, and efficacy was only described as "consistent with" IV monotherapy.
  Minchom A, Cho BC, Leighl NB et al. *Clin Lung Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41807198/
- **Phase II study of ramucirumab plus erlotinib for treatment-naïve patients with EGFR-mutant non-squamous non-small cell lung cancer and pleural effusion (RELAY-Effusion).**: Single-arm multicentre phase II trial (n=40) of first-line ramucirumab plus erlotinib in EGFR-mutant NSCLC with malignant pleural effusion. Median PFS was 12.9 months, so the PFS primary endpoint was **not met**. ORR was 77.5%, median OS 36.3 months and median drainage-free survival 33.0 months; grade 3-4 treatment-related AEs occurred in 22.5%. This adds phase 2 data for a poor-prognosis EGFR subgroup, though the authors frame the benefit mainly as control of the effusion. Limitations: small single-arm design, and the backbone is a first-generation TKI rather than current osimertinib-based standards.
  Kaneda H, Tachihara M, Toi Y et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42096800/
- **Asia-Pacific practical consensus in the management of adverse events related to amivantamab-based therapies in non-small cell lung cancer.**: Consensus guidance from a 12-clinician Asia-Pacific steering committee, based on a literature review and interviews with two patient advocacy groups. It recommends prophylactic anticoagulation, minocycline/doxycycline and moisturisers as key prevention for common amivantamab AEs, plus grade-based reactive management of skin, paronychia, mucositis, MET-related, VTE and infusion-reaction events. This is practical guidance for an approved EGFR-directed regimen. Limitation: expert consensus drawing on existing supportive-care evidence, not new trial data.
  Soo RA, Fernandez-Penas P, Li M et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42048802/
- **Durvalumab Plus Chemotherapy in Patients With EGFR-Mutated Advanced NSCLC Whose Disease Progressed on First-Line Osimertinib: ORCHARD.**: Module of the phase II biomarker-directed ORCHARD platform (n=25) testing durvalumab plus carboplatin-pemetrexed after progression on first-line osimertinib, in patients without a targetable resistance alteration. Confirmed ORR was 16%, median PFS 4.8 months and median OS 23.4 months; 36% had grade 3 or higher AEs. The investigators concluded that the risk-benefit profile did not justify further study. This is a negative post-osimertinib result. Limitation: very small single-arm module.
  Cho BC, Nishio M, Ahn MJ et al. *JTO Clin Res Rep*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42039685/
- **Mechanisms of Acquired Resistance Following Dual EGFR/MET Inhibition in MET-Amplified EGFR TKI-Resistant Lung Cancer.**: Analysis of 12 patients who developed resistance after dual EGFR/MET inhibition for MET-amplified EGFR-TKI resistance, with serial patient-derived cell lines. 42% acquired oncogenic fusions (3 BRAF, 1 ALK, 1 RET); single cases showed EGFR T790M, C797S or small-cell transformation. Some mechanisms were targetable: T790M and ALK fusion responded to subsequent targeted therapy, and osimertinib plus trametinib reversed BRAF-fusion resistance in vitro. Amivantamab induced ADCC in the resistant lines. Limitation: very small series, and the treatment implications rest largely on cell-line data.
  Lin YT, Liu YN, Hsu YC et al. *Mol Cancer Ther*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42008741/
- **Osimertinib after definitive CRT in unresectable stage III EGFR-mutated NSCLC: safety outcomes from the phase III LAURA study.**: Detailed safety report from the randomised phase III LAURA trial (n=216, 2:1 osimertinib vs placebo after definitive chemoradiotherapy). Radiation pneumonitis was more frequent with osimertinib (48% vs 38%) but mostly low grade; 87% of affected patients continued osimertinib. AEs led to discontinuation in 13% vs 5%, and ILD was mainly grade 1-2. This supports the tolerability of the consolidation standard in stage III EGFR-mutant disease. Limitation: safety-only report with unequal exposure between arms (median 24.0 vs 8.3 months); no new efficacy data.
  Kato T, Dong X, Takahashi T et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42296617/
- **Neoadjuvant sintilimab plus chemotherapy in EGFR-mutant non-small cell lung cancer (NEOTIDE/CTONG2104): phase II trial and correlative genomic analysis in China.**: Single-arm phase II trial (n=35) of 3 cycles of neoadjuvant sintilimab plus carboplatin/nab-paclitaxel in resectable stage II-IIIB EGFR-mutant NSCLC, followed by adjuvant osimertinib in stage III. The primary endpoint was met: MPR was 34.3%; pCR was 11.4%, ORR 60.0% and 2-year EFS 71.4%. Grade 3-4 AEs occurred in 29%, and response to later EGFR-TKIs appeared preserved. These are perioperative data in a subgroup where immunotherapy benefit is uncertain. Limitations: small single-arm study with no TKI-based comparator; exploratory genomic correlates (e.g. RB1/RBM10).
  Zhang C, Jiang BY, Yan LX et al. *EClinicalMedicine*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42256683/
- **Osimertinib plus selumetinib in patients with EGFR-mutated advanced NSCLC with BRAF alterations post-progression on first-line osimertinib: ORCHARD.**: Final results of the ORCHARD phase II platform module testing osimertinib plus the MEK inhibitor selumetinib in 16 patients with BRAF fusions or V600E after progression on first-line osimertinib. ORR was 7% (one partial response), median PFS 3.4 months and median OS 14.0 months, and 69% had grade >=3 adverse events. This is a negative result for one biomarker-matched post-osimertinib strategy; the authors conclude further evaluation is not warranted. The cohort was very small and single-arm.
  Piotrowska Z, Goldberg SB, Goldman JW et al. *Eur J Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42202477/
- **Osimertinib plus Gefitinib in Patients with EGFR-Mutated Advanced Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer and EGFR (C797X) Mutation Following First-Line Osimertinib: ORCHARD.**: Final data from the ORCHARD phase II module of osimertinib plus gefitinib in 31 patients with an acquired EGFR C797X mutation after first-line osimertinib. Confirmed ORR was 26% with median duration of response 4.2 months, median PFS 5.1 months and median OS 19 months; 35% had grade >=3 adverse events, and T790M, BRAF and PIK3CA alterations appeared at progression. The benefit was modest and the authors judge further evaluation not warranted, which bears on C797X-directed dual-TKI approaches. Single-arm and small.
  Goldberg SB, Ahn MJ, Baik C et al. *Clin Cancer Res*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42440354/
- **Durvalumab plus etoposide-platinum in patients with epidermal growth factor receptor (EGFR)-mutated advanced NSCLC and neuroendocrine transformation after first-line osimertinib: ORCHARD.**: Final ORCHARD phase 2 module of durvalumab plus etoposide-platinum in 14 patients whose tumours transformed to small cell or large cell neuroendocrine carcinoma after first-line osimertinib. Confirmed ORR was 43%, median duration of response 4.3 months, median PFS 4.2 months and median OS 10.2 months; 64% had grade >=3 adverse events. These are prospective data on treating histologic transformation, but responses were short-lived and the cohort is very small, with no comparator.
  Okamoto I, Cho BC, Goldberg SB et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42361644/
- **Phase Ib of repotrectinib plus osimertinib in patients with EGFR-mutated advanced non-small cell lung cancer.**: The phase Ib TOTEM trial (n=31; 17 with two or more prior lines) tested repotrectinib, which in preclinical work blocked Src/FAK signalling, plus osimertinib after progression on osimertinib. The recommended phase 2 dose was osimertinib 80 mg QD plus repotrectinib 160 mg BID. ORR was 22.2% with median PFS 4.0 months, intracranial ORR was 33.3%, and grade 3-4 treatment-related adverse events occurred in 45.2% (mainly dizziness and anaemia). This is an early-phase post-osimertinib combination in a heavily pretreated, biomarker-unselected population, so the efficacy estimates are imprecise.
  Aguilar A, Cobo M, Calles A et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42155306/
- **BCL-2/BCL-xL inhibitor pelcitoclax with osimertinib for EGFR-mutated advanced non-small-cell lung cancer: a phase 1b trial.**: A phase 1b trial (n=64) of weekly intravenous pelcitoclax plus osimertinib set the recommended phase 2 dose at 160 mg. After third-generation TKI plus chemotherapy (cohort 1, n=29), ORR was 10.7% and median PFS 2.7 months, with a trend to longer PFS when BCL-xL expression was high (4.2 vs 2.7 months, p=0.058). In TKI-naive patients (cohort 3, n=27), ORR was 80.8% and median PFS 16.4 months. Activity after resistance was limited, and the first-line cohort is small and has no comparator with osimertinib alone.
  Ma Y, Wu C, Zhao YQ et al. *J Immunother Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42442920/
- **High-dose furmonertinib as first-line treatment for untreated EGFR-mutated advanced NSCLC with central nervous system metastases: A phase 2 trial.**: A single-arm phase II trial gave furmonertinib 160 mg daily to 40 untreated patients with EGFR-mutant NSCLC and CNS metastases. At a median follow-up of 18.66 months, blinded independent review found median PFS of 16.59 months and median intracranial PFS of 17.83 months, with systemic ORR 92.5% and intracranial ORR 95.0%; 15% had grade >=3 treatment-related adverse events. Exploratory paired plasma/CSF profiling showed high EGFR mutation clearance. These are relevant CNS efficacy data, but the trial was small, had no comparator and has short follow-up for OS.
  Yan H, Jiang W, Xiong Y et al. *Cell Rep Med*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42379171/
- **Clinical Significance of EGFR Amplification in Patients with EGFR-Mutated Metastatic Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Receiving First-line Osimertinib.**: A retrospective cohort of 473 patients treated with first-line osimertinib who had baseline NGS, 17.1% of whom had EGFR amplification (copy number >=6). Amplification was linked to more TP53 co-mutations and brain/liver/bone metastases, and to shorter PFS (11.6 vs 19 months, HR 1.77) and OS (34 vs 40.1 months, HR 1.40) despite a similar response rate. Among the 113 patients rebiopsied after resistance, those with amplification more often had acquired MET alterations (29% vs 12%). This points to a candidate baseline marker for choosing intensified first-line treatment, but the design is retrospective and the marker has not been validated prospectively.
  Di Federico A, Pecci F, Jeng M et al. *Clin Cancer Res*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42446521/

### lung-key-evidence

- **Serplulimab Plus Chemotherapy, with or without HLX04, versus Chemotherapy as First-Line Treatment for Nonsquamous NSCLC: Final Survival Analysis of the Phase III ASTRUM-002 Study.**: Final OS analysis of the double-blind phase III ASTRUM-002 trial (n=636, 1:1:1) in first-line nonsquamous NSCLC without EGFR or ALK/ROS1 alterations. Serplulimab plus chemotherapy improved OS vs chemotherapy (26.8 vs 20.3 months, HR 0.66; crossover-adjusted HR 0.53-0.65). Adding the bevacizumab biosimilar HLX04 gave no further benefit (23.7 months; HR 1.12 vs serplulimab plus chemotherapy). These are phase 3 immunotherapy results relevant to lung-key-evidence; the trial excluded EGFR-mutant patients. Limitation: 37.6% of the control arm crossed over to serplulimab plus HLX04, so the OS comparison relies partly on crossover-adjusted models.
  Hao X, Wang L, Hao Y et al. *Cancer Commun (Lond)*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42282892/
- **SAFFRON-301: a randomised phase III study of sitravatinib in combination with tislelizumab in patients with locally advanced or metastatic non-small cell lung cancer**: Open-label phase 3 (n=377) of sitravatinib plus tislelizumab vs docetaxel after platinum chemotherapy and anti-PD-(L)1. Median OS was identical (11.5 vs 11.4 months, HR 1.02) and PFS was not significantly improved (4.4 vs 2.9 months, HR 0.82, 95% CI 0.62-1.07). A negative trial, closing off this combination after immunotherapy failure; follow-up was short (about 11.5 months).
  Zhou Q, Gao B, Hu J et al. *BMJ Oncol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41737914/
- **Efficacy and Safety of Ultra-Low-Dose Immunotherapy in Relapsed Refractory Solid Tumors: Phase III Superiority Randomized Trial (DELII)**: Indian phase 3 (n=500; 36% lung cancer) of nivolumab 20 mg every 2 weeks vs docetaxel/paclitaxel after at least one prior line. Median OS was 5.88 vs 4.70 months (HR 0.80, P=.022), with less grade ≥3 toxicity (42.5% vs 60.8%) and better QoL; PFS was the same. Mixed tumour types, so the lung-specific benefit is not shown in the abstract, and the comparator is chemotherapy rather than standard-dose immunotherapy; relevant mainly to access where standard doses are unaffordable.
  Noronha V, Patil V, Menon N et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41604598/
- **Four-Year Outcomes for Nivolumab With Chemotherapy and Bevacizumab in Patients With Nonsquamous NSCLC in the TASUKI-52**: Final 4-year analysis of a phase 3 trial (n=550) of nivolumab vs placebo added to carboplatin-paclitaxel-bevacizumab first line. The OS benefit persisted (HR 0.71; 4-year OS 34.7% vs 22.1%) and PFS improved (HR 0.61); there were no new safety signals. The population was Asian, and the abstract reports no EGFR-mutant subgroup data.
  Lee JS, Sugawara S, Kang JH et al. *Cancer Sci*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41611341/
- **Clinical outcomes with perioperative nivolumab by nodal status in patients with stage III resectable NSCLC: phase 3 CheckMate 77T exploratory analysis**: Exploratory analysis of the phase 3 CheckMate 77T trial by nodal status. In N2 disease, perioperative nivolumab improved event-free survival (1-year 70% vs 45%, HR 0.46) and pCR (22.0% vs 5.6%), including in multistation N2 (HR 0.43), with similar rates of definitive surgery. Exploratory subgroup with immature follow-up and no OS data yet.
  Provencio M, Awad MM, Spicer JD et al. *Nat Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41507539/
- **Ten-year overall survival in resectable stage-III NSCLC - Results of the randomized ESPATUE trial - Long-term survival and competing risk analysis**: 10-year update of a randomised trial (n=161) of surgery vs boost chemoradiotherapy after induction chemotherapy and chemoradiotherapy in operable stage III NSCLC. 10-year OS was 29.9% vs 28.3% (p=0.70), with no difference in competing causes of death. Small trial from the pre-immunotherapy era that closed early, so it cannot show equivalence.
  Eberhardt WEE, Schulte C, Pöttgen C et al. *Eur J Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41702246/
- **The clinical and cost-effectiveness of paravertebral blockade versus thoracic epidural blockade in reducing chronic post-thoracotomy pain: TOPIC2 RCT synopsis**: UK multicentre RCT (n=770, 15 centres) of paravertebral vs thoracic epidural block for thoracotomy. Chronic pain at 6 months was 22% vs 16% (adjusted RR 1.32, 95% CI 0.93-1.86, p=0.12), and paravertebral block caused less hypotension but more day-1 pain; cost-effectiveness was similar. Enrolment fell short of target (power 80% instead of 90%) and the trial was unblinded.
  Shelley B, Middleton L, Goebel A et al. *Health Technol Assess*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41664616/
- **NCCN Guidelines® Insights: Small Cell Lung Cancer, Version 2.2026**: Summary of the latest updates to the NCCN SCLC guideline, focused on changes in systemic therapy and radiotherapy. The abstract does not list the specific changes.
  Ganti AKP, Loo BW, Badiyan S et al. *J Natl Compr Canc Netw*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41671459/
- **ACR Appropriateness Criteria® Radiologic Management of Pulmonary Nodules and Masses: Update 2025**: Updated ACR guideline on imaging and procedures (biopsy, PET/CT, ablation, SBRT, surgery) for pulmonary nodules and masses across clinical scenarios. It emphasises tissue sampling for enlarging or suspicious nodules and a selective role for PET/CT. Guideline built from literature review plus expert consensus, with no new data.
  Expert Panel on Interventional Radiology, Gilyard S, Ahmed O et al. *J Am Coll Radiol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41721805/
- **China National Lung Cancer Screening Guideline with Low-dose Computed Tomography (2025 Version)**: Revision of the Chinese LDCT screening guideline. It specifies occupational exposure criteria, uses mean nodule diameter, raises the baseline positive threshold for solid nodules to 6 mm with 3-6-month follow-up, and adds shared decision-making. National guideline (article in Chinese) with no new trial data.
  Chinese Expert Group on Early Diagnosis and Treatment of Lung Cancer, China Lung Oncology Group. *Zhongguo Fei Ai Za Zhi*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41565348/
- **Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer, Version 4.2026, NCCN Clinical Practice Guidelines In Oncology.**: Guideline update from the NCCN NSCLC panel, focusing on treatment recommendations for advanced or metastatic NSCLC with actionable biomarkers. The abstract states that the list of recommended targeted therapies has been updated based on recent FDA approvals and clinical data, but it does not name the specific changes. This matters as a guideline update covering driver-positive disease, including EGFR. Limitation: the abstract does not describe what changed, so the full text must be checked.
  Riely GJ, Wood DE, Aisner DL et al. *J Natl Compr Canc Netw*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41956107/
- **Early repetitive transcranial magnetic stimulation for preventing chronic postoperative pain in older adults: a randomized clinical sub-study.**: Randomised trial in 230 older adults undergoing thoracoscopic lung cancer surgery, comparing active with sham rTMS given immediately after extubation (198 completed 3-month follow-up). Chronic postsurgical pain occurred in 24.3% vs 43.5% (RR 0.56, 95% CI 0.39-0.80), with better anxiety and depression scores at 3 months. This is sham-controlled evidence for a non-drug perioperative intervention in lung cancer surgery. Limitations: single trial (described as a sub-study), 14% loss to follow-up, and only 3 months of follow-up; the CXCL10 biomarker findings are exploratory.
  Zhou WW, Yang Z, Zhou C et al. *BMC Med*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41943114/
- **Efficacy and safety of savolitinib in Chinese patients with locally advanced or metastatic MET exon 14-mutated non-small cell lung cancer: final results of a confirmatory Phase 3b study.**: Final results of a single-arm, open-label confirmatory phase 3b at 48 Chinese hospitals (n=166) of savolitinib in METex14-skipping NSCLC. Independent-review ORR was 42% in previously treated patients (n=79) and 62% in treatment-naive patients (n=87). Median PFS was 13.7 months in both cohorts, and median OS was 25.3 and 28.3 months. Grade >=3 treatment-related adverse events occurred in 62%. These are mature targeted-therapy data in a driver subgroup. Limitations: no comparator arm, Chinese population only, and industry funded.
  Yu Y, Guo Q, Zhang Y et al. *Lancet Reg Health West Pac*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41938023/
- **ESTRO-EORTC expert guideline on target delineation and radiotherapy details for stage I-III small cell lung cancer.**: Updated European expert guideline from ESTRO with the EORTC Lung Cancer Group on radiotherapy for stage I-III SCLC. It recommends PET-CT and brain MRI staging, early thoracic RT (45 Gy/30 BID or 66 Gy/33 once daily) with 4-6 cycles of platinum-etoposide, then consolidation durvalumab for up to two years in fit patients. PCI remains standard after response, and hippocampal sparing and MRI surveillance are still under study. This is a new consensus guideline for limited-stage SCLC. Limitation: it is expert consensus, and several areas (DLL3 agents, biomarker-guided care) are explicitly marked as investigational.
  Levy A, Le Péchoux C, Grønberg B et al. *Radiother Oncol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41932500/
- **[Chinese Expert Consensus on Clinical Management of Oncogene Addicted Non-small Cell Lung Cancer with Leptomeningeal Metastasis (2026 Edition)].**: Multidisciplinary Chinese expert consensus on diagnosing and treating leptomeningeal metastasis in driver-positive NSCLC. It recommends diagnosis by symptoms, MRI and CSF cytology, CSF liquid biopsy for driver status and response, and high-CNS-penetration TKIs as first-line therapy, with intrathecal chemotherapy and radiotherapy as supplementary measures. This is relevant to CNS management of EGFR-mutant and other oncogene-driven disease. Limitations: it is a national expert consensus rather than new trial data, and the English abstract does not give evidence grades or name specific drugs.
  Lung Cancer Medical Education Committee of Chinese Medical Education Association et al. *Zhongguo Fei Ai Za Zhi*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41916910/
- **A Subgroup Analysis of Perioperative Pembrolizumab in Clinical Stage II Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer from the Randomized KEYNOTE-671 Study.**: Exploratory analysis of the 239 participants with clinical stage II disease among 797 randomised in the phase 3 KEYNOTE-671 trial (perioperative pembrolizumab vs placebo plus neoadjuvant chemotherapy), at a median follow-up of 49.9 months. EFS improved (HR 0.50, 95% CI 0.34-0.74), as did MPR (+25.7%) and pCR (+21.3%). OS HR was 0.69 (95% CI 0.43-1.11), which is not statistically significant. Grade 3-4 TRAEs occurred in 50.0% vs 40.5%. This is perioperative immunotherapy evidence in stage II disease. Limitation: an exploratory subgroup analysis not powered for OS.
  Tsuboi M, Wakelee H, Garassino MC et al. *Eur J Cardiothorac Surg*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41875364/
- **Randomised trial of delivering co-located, personalised stop-smoking support within lung cancer screening: the YESS study.**: Randomised trial of 1,003 people who smoked and attended UK lung cancer screening, comparing standard best-practice cessation support with an added personalised package (a booklet of the participant's own CT images and scripted practitioner communication). Validated 7-day abstinence did not differ at 3 months (33.6% vs 30.0%, OR 1.17, 95% CI 0.90-1.54) or 12 months (29.2% vs 28.6%). A subgroup benefit was seen in women at 3 months (OR 1.70). This is negative screening-programme RCT evidence that informs how cessation is built into screening. Limitations: the sex difference is a subgroup finding, and both arms received intensive co-located support.
  Murray RL, Baldwin D, Brain K et al. *Thorax*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41825863/
- **First-Line Tislelizumab Plus Chemotherapy for Advanced or Metastatic Squamous Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: 4-Year Long-Term Follow-Up from RATIONALE-307.**: Four-year update of the phase 3 RATIONALE-307 trial of tislelizumab plus paclitaxel/carboplatin (A) or nab-paclitaxel/carboplatin (B) vs paclitaxel/carboplatin alone (C) in untreated advanced squamous NSCLC, at a median OS follow-up of 44.8 months. Median OS was 26.1, 23.3 and 19.4 months, with 4-year OS of 32.2%, 26.0% and 19.2%. Median PFS was 7.7, 9.5 and 5.5 months. These are long-term phase 3 immunotherapy data. Limitations: 58.7% crossover from the chemotherapy arm, grade >=3 TEAEs in about 89% of patients, and a mainly Chinese population.
  Wang Z, Yu X, Zhao J et al. *Oncol Ther*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41811656/
- **Nivolumab plus chemoradiotherapy followed by nivolumab with or without ipilimumab for untreated locally advanced stage III NSCLC: a randomized phase 3 trial.**: Randomised phase 3 in untreated, unresectable stage III NSCLC comparing nivolumab plus concurrent chemoradiotherapy followed by nivolumab plus ipilimumab (A) or nivolumab alone (B) with CCRT followed by durvalumab (C). At a median follow-up of 30.5 months, PFS did not differ for A vs C (HR 0.95, 96% CI 0.77-1.19) or B vs C (HR 0.84, 95% CI 0.69-1.04), and descriptive OS showed no improvement. Both nivolumab arms had more pneumonitis. This negative phase 3 leaves CCRT followed by durvalumab as the comparator to beat. Limitation: the abstract does not report arm sizes or biomarker subgroups.
  Peters S, Tan DSW, Gerber DE et al. *Nat Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42129521/
- **Final overall survival analysis of the APPLE study: atezolizumab and platinum-pemetrexed with or without bevacizumab for metastatic nonsquamous non-small cell lung cancer.**: Final OS analysis (about 3.5 years of follow-up) of the phase 3 APPLE trial (n=411) adding bevacizumab to atezolizumab plus carboplatin-pemetrexed in advanced nonsquamous NSCLC. Median OS was 28.0 vs 25.7 months (HR 0.88, 95% CI 0.70-1.10), with no benefit in driver-negative patients (HR 0.96). In the 124 driver-positive patients, median OS was 28.0 vs 20.8 months (HR 0.71, 95% CI 0.47-1.08), a non-significant trend. This is relevant to post-TKI options in oncogene-driven (including EGFR-mutant) disease. Limitation: the driver-positive result is a subgroup finding with a CI crossing 1, and the abstract does not break it down by driver type.
  Shiraishi Y, Kishimoto J, Sugawara S et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42127535/
- **The 2026 American Association for Thoracic Surgery Expert Consensus Document: Evaluation and management of N2+ non-small cell lung cancer.**: An international multidisciplinary AATS panel used a modified Delphi process after a librarian-assisted literature review and reached consensus on 13 statements on staging, what makes disease unresectable, use of chemoimmunotherapy or targeted therapy, neoadjuvant and/or adjuvant therapy, restaging, and whether resection is safe after chemoimmunotherapy in potentially resectable N2 NSCLC. It matters because it is a new consensus document for a setting where practice is changing quickly. Its main limitation is that it rests on expert consensus, and the abstract does not give the individual recommendations or their evidence levels.
  Linden PA, Martin LW, Stiles B et al. *J Thorac Cardiovasc Surg*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42081972/
- **Five-year overall survival in JCOG1205/1206: irinotecan or etoposide plus cisplatin for resected high-grade neuroendocrine carcinoma of the lung.**: This randomized open-label phase 3 trial (n=221) compared adjuvant irinotecan-cisplatin (IP) with etoposide-cisplatin (EP) after complete resection of stage I-IIIA high-grade neuroendocrine lung carcinoma. The updated analysis, 5 years after the last enrolment, still showed no difference: 5-year RFS was 65.7% with EP and 65.2% with IP (HR 1.026, 95% CI 0.670-1.569), and 5-year OS was 73.5% and 72.4% (HR 1.175, 95% CI 0.742-1.861). This is long-term phase 3 adjuvant evidence that supports EP as the standard and gives no reason to switch to IP. One limitation is that local and central pathology review agreed on the diagnosis in only 75.6% of cases, which matters in a mixed group of tumours.
  Kenmotsu H, Niho S, Tsuboi M et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42000139/
- **The Society of Thoracic Surgeons Expert Consensus Document (2026) on Addressing Definition and Practices of Sublobar Resection in Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer.**: After two randomized trials showed sublobar resection to be noninferior to lobectomy for node-negative NSCLC of 2 cm or less, an STS panel of thoracic surgeons used a modified Delphi method (three voting rounds, threshold above 75% agreement) to address 7 areas of controversy. It agreed on 21 statements on how to define sublobar resection and when to use it in early-stage NSCLC. This guides how practice-changing trial results are applied in surgery. It is expert consensus, and the abstract does not give the content of the statements.
  Khullar OV, Kidane B, Altorki N et al. *Ann Thorac Surg*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42320552/
- **[Chinese Expert Consensus on Delta-like Ligand 3-T Cell Engager Therapy for SCLC].**: The SCLC Expert Committee of the Chinese Society of Clinical Oncology convened a panel to write a consensus on DLL3-targeted T-cell engagers, with tarlatamab named as an approved example of the class. It aims to standardize their clinical use in China: mechanism, suitable patients and clinical management. It is relevant because it is among the first structured guidance for a new treatment class in SCLC. Its limitations are that it is national, built from evidence plus expert experience, and only the abstract (a translated summary) was available, with no specific recommendations.
  Small Cell Lung Cancer Expert Committee of the Chinese Society of Clinical Oncology. *Zhongguo Fei Ai Za Zhi*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42290048/
- **SEPAR-SECT Recommendations for Perioperative Invasive Mediastinal Staging of Non-small Cell Lung Cancer.**: This joint consensus from the Spanish societies SEPAR and SECT updates the 2011 SEPAR guidance to the 9th edition of TNM and now covers staging both before and during surgery. It was built from a structured literature review plus multidisciplinary expert consensus. Key recommendations: no invasive mediastinal staging is needed for T1a-b tumours without radiological nodal disease, whatever their location; N1 disease is treated as high-risk for hidden mediastinal involvement; and when suspicion is intermediate, surgical staging is more accurate than endosonography alone. It is a national expert-consensus document, and the abstract does not report formal evidence grading.
  Call S, Botana-Rial M, Cordovilla R et al. *Arch Bronconeumol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42276952/
- **ERS/ESGE/ESTS clinical practice guidelines on endobronchial and oesophageal endosonography for the diagnosis and staging of lung cancer.**: A joint task force of three European societies (ERS, ESGE, ESTS) answered 12 guideline questions using systematic searches (to April 2025) and GRADE methods. Its recommendations: use endosonography rather than mediastinoscopy for mediastinal staging and for restaging after induction therapy; make systematic staging the minimum standard; ideally combine EBUS-TBNA with EUS(-B)-FNA; and do not add mediastinoscopy after a negative endosonography. It also finds the evidence too thin for routine cryobiopsy and rates EBUS-TBNA samples well suited for PD-L1 testing. Several recommendations are only "suggested" (conditional), and the abstract does not report the certainty of the evidence.
  Korevaar DA, Kovacevic B, Papadopoulou E et al. *Eur Respir J*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42167778/
- **Surgical Outcomes of Perioperative Toripalimab in Stage III Resectable Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: Post Hoc Analysis of the Neotorch Randomized Clinical Trial.**: This post hoc analysis of the double-blind phase 3 Neotorch RCT (404 patients with stage IIIA-IIIB NSCLC, 314 operated, 50 Chinese centres) looked at surgical outcomes. Fewer patients cancelled surgery with toripalimab plus chemotherapy than with placebo plus chemotherapy (17.8% vs 26.7%). Tumour downstaging (80.7% vs 50.7%) and nodal downstaging (67.5% vs 48.6%) were higher, and surgical complications were similar. Downstaging was associated with better EFS. This supports surgical feasibility after perioperative chemo-immunotherapy. Limitations: the analysis was post hoc, median follow-up was short (18.3 months), and the population was 90% male and Chinese only.
  Fang W, Wang Y, Wang W et al. *JAMA Surg*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42455561/
- **Evaluation of Biomarkers for Sacituzumab Govitecan in Metastatic Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: Insights From the EVOKE-01 Study.**: This exploratory biomarker analysis of the open-label phase 3 EVOKE-01 trial compared the Trop-2 ADC sacituzumab govitecan with docetaxel in previously treated NSCLC. Trop-2 expression did not predict benefit: OS HR was 0.96 with Trop-2 at or above the median and 0.89 below it. DDR alterations also did not predict benefit. ctDNA fell after cycle 1 in both arms, and TP53-mutant patients appeared to benefit more from the ADC. The analysis is relevant to how Trop-2 ADCs are developed in NSCLC. However, it is exploratory and based on biomarker-evaluable subsets of a trial that was negative overall.
  Felip E, Kuo P, Garrido P et al. *Cancer Res Commun*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42447505/
- **Neoadjuvant and perioperative chemo-immunotherapy in early-stage non-small cell lung cancer: international expert panel meeting by AIOT.**: Seven international thoracic oncology experts met under the Italian Association of Thoracic Oncology (AIOT) and wrote shared-opinion statements on resectable NSCLC. They endorsed pathological mediastinal staging and molecular testing before therapy. They called neoadjuvant or perioperative chemo-immunotherapy a new standard for resectable stage II-III disease, especially stage III and N-positive, while upfront surgery could be considered for selected stage II N0 cases. They found no evidence favouring the perioperative over the neoadjuvant approach, or the reverse. This is a consensus statement from a small panel built through moderated discussion, not a formal systematic guideline process.
  de Marinis F, Cascone T, Forde PM et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42470746/
- **ESTRO clinical practice guideline on high-dose thoracic reirradiation for non-small cell lung cancer.**: An international ESTRO panel used a modified Delphi process to produce 42 consensus recommendations across eight key questions. These cover patient selection, staging, target delineation, dose and fractionation, cumulative dose assessment, combination with systemic therapy, and follow-up for high-dose thoracic reirradiation in NSCLC. Highly conformal image-guided delivery and structured cumulative dose assessment were considered essential. Guidance on combining reirradiation with systemic therapy remains conditional. This is a new European guideline, but it rests mainly on expert consensus because high-quality randomised evidence is lacking.
  Andratschke N, Willmann J, Ippolito E et al. *Radiother Oncol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42468604/
- **Program Guidelines for the National Lung Cancer Screening Program: Targeted Lung Cancer Screening in High-Risk Individuals in Australia.**: This paper summarises the guidelines for Australia's National Lung Cancer Screening Program, launched in July 2025. The program offers risk-based low-dose CT screening to people aged 50-70 with a history of cigarette smoking. Healthcare providers must offer smoking cessation support throughout the pathway, and scans are assessed under a national nodule management protocol. It is a new national screening guideline that sets out how the program is delivered. It reports no outcome data, so effectiveness and equity in practice are still to be shown.
  Rankin NM, Zosel R, Whop LJ et al. *Med J Aust*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42438375/
- **SEPAR-SECT recommendations for perioperative invasive mediastinal staging of non-small cell lung cancer.**: This joint consensus from the Spanish pulmonology and thoracic surgery societies updates the 2011 SEPAR guidelines to the 9th-edition TNM. It covers both preoperative and intraoperative mediastinal staging and includes a staging algorithm based on risk. According to the recommendations, T1a-b tumours without radiological nodal disease need no invasive staging. N1 disease is flagged as high risk for occult mediastinal involvement. For intermediate suspicion, surgical staging is preferred over endosonography alone. It is a national consensus built from a structured literature review and expert agreement, not new trial data.
  Call S, Botana-Rial M, Cordovilla R et al. *Cir Esp (Engl Ed)*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42373333/
- **Chinese expert consensus on radiotherapy combined with immunotherapy for unresectable lung cancer (2026 Edition).**: Several Chinese societies (CACA committees, CSCO SCLC committee and the CMA respiratory lung cancer group) updated their 2024 consensus on combining radiotherapy with immunotherapy for unresectable NSCLC and SCLC. They reviewed recent evidence and went through several rounds of revision. The abstract gives no specific recommendations or levels of evidence, so its practical content cannot be judged from the abstract. It is a regional consensus statement, not new data.
  Wang L, Liu Y, Zhao W et al. *Cancer Lett*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42413815/

### lung-nonpharm

- **Leisure-Time Physical Activity and Cancer Mortality Among Cancer Survivors**: Pooled analysis of 6 US prospective cohorts (17,141 survivors of 7 cancers, mean follow-up 10.9 years) relating moderate-to-vigorous physical activity after diagnosis to cancer mortality. Among lung cancer survivors, even low activity (>0 to <7.5 vs 0 MET-h/wk) was associated with lower cancer mortality (HR 0.56), and meeting guidelines after diagnosis, even if inactive before, was associated with HR 0.58 (95% CI 0.47-0.71). Observational design with self-reported activity measured years after diagnosis, so reverse causation (sicker patients being less active) cannot be excluded; baseline data are from 1976-1997, before modern lung cancer treatment.
  Rees-Punia E, Teras LR, Newton CC et al. *JAMA Netw Open*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41701497/
- **Effects of 2 Rehabilitation Protocols on Pulmonary Function in Lung Cancer Patients After Thoracoscopic Surgery: A Randomized Controlled Trial of Baduanjin Versus Bedside Cycle Ergometer Training**: Single-centre RCT (n=520) after VATS for lung cancer comparing Baduanjin (qigong) training with bedside cycle ergometer training. The Baduanjin group had higher FEV1 (2.53 vs 2.00 L) and FVC, lower pain and fatigue scores and lower inflammatory markers (all P<.001). Single centre, unblinded, short-term outcomes only, and the very large FEV1 difference between arms warrants caution.
  Lu L, Shuhuan Y, Zuyuan Z et al. *Eur J Cardiothorac Surg*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41632620/
- **Nurse-supported hybrid home-based pulmonary rehabilitation improves psychological distress, quality of life, and functional performance in advanced lung cancer: A randomized controlled trial**: RCT of 104 patients with stage III-IV lung cancer comparing an 8-week home-based aerobic, resistance and breathing programme with usual care. At week 8 anxiety (β -1.72) and depression (β -1.21) fell more in the intervention arm, and QoL and function were maintained while they declined in controls; no serious exercise-related events, including in patients with bone metastases. Small, single-country trial with 63.5% adherence and 8 weeks of follow-up.
  Liu WL, Chien JY, Lu YY et al. *Eur J Oncol Nurs*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41666769/
- **Randomized controlled trial: Synergistic effect of three-ball mindfulness breathing rehabilitation training following pulmonary lobectomy for lung cancer**: Single-centre RCT (n=124) adding three-ball mindfulness breathing training to standard pulmonary rehabilitation after lobectomy. The intervention shortened sputum retention and hospital stay and improved dyspnoea, 6MWT, distress and fatigue scores, but complications did not differ significantly (9.7% vs 17.7%). Small, unblinded, single-centre study with follow-up only to one week after discharge.
  Hong Y, Lin Q, Wang S et al. *Support Care Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41739224/
- **The Society of Thoracic Surgeons (2025) Expert Consensus Document on Interventions for Screen-Detected Lung Nodules**: Modified-Delphi STS consensus (23 statements) on surgery and biopsy for screen-detected nodules. It accepts surgery without tissue diagnosis in selected patients, rejects pneumonectomy without diagnosis, sets complication benchmarks (e.g. surgical 30-day mortality <1%) and recommends resection within 12 weeks of the suspicious scan. Consensus, not new trial data.
  Servais EL, Hayanga JWA, Linden P et al. *Ann Thorac Surg*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41633462/
- **Intelligent Breathing Training Using a Digital Device for Postoperative Lung Cancer Patients: A Randomized Controlled Trial.**: Single-centre randomised trial of 138 patients after partial lung resection, comparing device-guided breathing training plus routine care with routine care alone. The intervention group had better FEV1, FVC, FEV1/FVC and peak expiratory flow at discharge, 1 and 3 months (all p<0.05), and longer 6-minute walk distance and better WHOQOL-100 physical scores (p<0.001). This is randomised evidence for a non-drug intervention improving postoperative function and quality of life. Limitations: single centre, unblinded, only 3 months of follow-up, and no survival or complication endpoints.
  Chen L, Liu J, Xia H et al. *Biol Res Nurs*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41506643/
- **Long-Term Effects of High-Intensity Training after Lung Cancer Surgery-A 5-Yr Follow-Up of a Randomized Controlled Trial.**: Long-term follow-up of a randomised trial of 20 weeks of high-intensity training (HIT) vs standard care started shortly after lung cancer surgery. Of 61 completers, 40 were alive at 5 years and 32 were retested. There was no between-group difference in peak oxygen uptake (adjusted difference 1.13 mL/kg/min, p=0.576), muscle mass, pulmonary function or QoL. Leg-press strength stayed higher with HIT (+23 kg, p=0.048), and 5-year survival was similar (67% vs 65%), with no 10-year difference. This is informative null randomised evidence on long-term benefits of postoperative exercise. Limitations: small numbers at follow-up and no power for survival.
  Edvardsen E, Anderssen SA, Borchsenius F et al. *Med Sci Sports Exerc*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41851742/
- **A multidisciplinary comfort-enhanced recovery after surgery program improves perioperative patient-reported outcomes after lung cancer surgery: a randomized controlled trial.**: Single-centre, single-blind randomised trial of 134 lung cancer surgery patients (121 completed 1 month), comparing a comfort-enhanced ERAS protocol (multimodal analgesia, minimal tubes, early mobilisation, TCM interventions, psychological support) with standard ERAS. Comfort scores were higher at discharge (+1.05, p=0.01) and at 1 month (+0.57, p=0.006), with lower cough, pain, sleep and fatigue burden and better emotional function, QoL and satisfaction. This is randomised evidence for a multicomponent non-drug perioperative programme. Limitations: single centre, a bundled intervention (component effects cannot be separated), short follow-up, and no complication or survival endpoints reported.
  Li H, Chen L, Zhou L et al. *J Thorac Dis*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41816488/
- **Association of Sleep-Related Hypoxemia With Survival in Patients With Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: The NEOSAS Groupe Français de Pneumo-Cancérologie Study Group.**: This multicentre prospective cohort enrolled 1,201 patients with newly diagnosed NSCLC (ECOG-PS 0-2) at 27 sites, and 1,001 had a valid home sleep study and 18 months of follow-up. OSA (38%) was not associated with survival. Severe sustained nocturnal hypoxaemia was linked to higher mortality after adjustment (HR 1.35 for T90 ≥35% vs <4%; HR 1.48 for mean SpO2 <91% vs ≥93%). This is a large prospective dataset on sleep in lung cancer, but it is observational, so it cannot show that treating hypoxaemia (e.g. with oxygen) would improve survival. Only 11% of patients had stage I-II disease.
  Justeau G, Greillier L, Vinas F et al. *Chest*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41812988/

## Medium

- Spatial single-cell proteotyping reveals immunotherapy-resistant features within the complex tumor microenvironment of metastatic NSCLC. (J Clin Invest). Biomarker study of 103 metastatic NSCLC patients (81 ICI-treated) using 29-marker spatial IHC; mechanistic with a clinical angle, including a suggested CD73-mediated ICI tolerance in EGFR/ALK+ NSCLC. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41805727/
- Cardiotoxic Effects of Osimertinib Compared to Other EGFR Inhibitors: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis. (Cardiovasc Toxicol). Systematic review and meta-analysis of 5 studies (19,008 patients) on osimertinib cardiotoxicity versus other EGFR TKIs; relevant safety evidence, though based on few, partly observational studies. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41801573/
- The STAT3-ZEB1 axis contributes to CCL2-mediated resistance to osimertinib in lung cancer. (Front Oncol). Preclinical study (patient pleural effusions, cell lines, xenografts) proposing a CCL2-STAT3/ERK-ZEB1 axis of osimertinib resistance; mechanistic with a possible clinical angle. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41800052/
- Clinicopathological and genomic features of early-onset non-small cell lung cancer across age and ethnicity. (Int J Cancer). Large genomic/clinical comparison of early-onset NSCLC across China (n=1948), MSK (n=6220) and a real-world cohort (n=3914), showing more actionable drivers including EGFR in young patients. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41793321/
- Clinical Utility of Next-Generation Sequencing in Tumors Diagnosed as Lung Squamous Cell Carcinoma: Real-World Data of Diagnostic and Therapeutic Implications. (Mod Pathol). Real-world institutional cohort of 576 tumours diagnosed as lung squamous cell carcinoma showing the diagnostic and therapeutic yield of NGS, including reclassified transformed adenocarcinomas. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41786210/
- Osimertinib after definitive chemoradiotherapy in patients with unresectable stage III EGFR-mutated NSCLC: LAURA China cohort. (Lung Cancer). Prespecified exploratory China-cohort subgroup analysis of the phase III LAURA trial of osimertinib after chemoradiotherapy; supportive of the main result but a subgroup. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41785639/
- Single-cell profiling of tumor lineage plasticity and the immune microenvironment in transformed small cell lung cancer. (J Transl Med). Single-cell RNA-seq of transformed SCLC (12 patients, 3 transformed) exploring lineage plasticity in histologic transformation; mechanistic but very small. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41781968/
- ctDNA clearance predicts survival in unresectable EGFR-mutant NSCLC: a meta-analysis. (Front Oncol). Meta-analysis of 4 prospective studies (336 patients) linking ctDNA clearance on EGFR-TKI therapy to survival in unresectable EGFR-mutant NSCLC; relevant to ctDNA-guided strategies but small. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41777653/
- Beamion PANTUMOR-1: rationale and design of a Phase II trial of zongertinib in HER2-overexpressed/amplified or HER2-mutant solid tumors. (Future Oncol). Design paper for the phase II Beamion PANTUMOR-1 basket trial of zongertinib in HER2-altered solid tumours; protocol of a notable trial, no results. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41777164/
- Early brain radiotherapy combined with third-generation EGFR-TKIs improves survival in EGFR-mutant NSCLC with synchronous brain metastases: a multi-center retrospective analysis. (Front Oncol). Multicentre retrospective comparison (n=83 vs 83 vs 27) suggesting early brain radiotherapy with first-line 3rd-generation EGFR TKIs improves iPFS (22.4 vs 15.7 months) and OS versus deferred radiotherapy; CNS-relevant but non-randomised. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41768243/
- Prognostic impact and treatment options for EGFR-mutant non-small cell lung cancer with concurrent EGFR amplification. (Lung Cancer). Retrospective cohort of 781 patients assessing the prognostic impact of concurrent EGFR amplification and TKI choice in EGFR-mutant NSCLC, with IPTW adjustment. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41763064/
- TGF-β Inhibitor Potentiates Osimertinib-Induced Anti-Tumor Immunity in Egfr-Mutant Lung Cancer. (Cancer Sci). Syngeneic mouse study showing TGF-beta inhibition enhances osimertinib-induced antitumour immunity in Egfr-mutant lung cancer; mechanistic with a combination-therapy angle. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41757675/
- The Impact of HER3 Dynamics Altered by HER3-DXd Alone and in Combination with Driver Oncogene Inhibitors on HER3-DXd Efficacy. (Int J Mol Sci). Preclinical study of how HER3 expression changes under driver-oncogene inhibitors affect HER3-DXd (patritumab deruxtecan) efficacy; relevant to combination strategies with ADCs. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41752065/
- Survival analysis and prognostic factors in advanced NSCLC harboring EGFR PACC mutations: A multicenter retrospective study. (Lung Cancer). Multicentre retrospective cohort of 141 patients with EGFR PACC mutations, screened from 6,842, reporting real-world OS and prognostic factors for this uncommon subgroup. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41747608/
- Cost Effectiveness of Osimertinib with Chemotherapy Compared to Osimertinib Monotherapy and First-Generation EGFR-TKIs in Advanced NSCLC in the USA. (Clin Drug Investig). US cost-effectiveness model of osimertinib plus chemotherapy (FLAURA2) versus osimertinib monotherapy and 1st-generation TKIs; relevant to access. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41746542/
- Patient-derived malignant effusion organoids guide tailored therapy and dissect third-generation EGFR-tyrosine kinase inhibitor resistance mechanisms in lung cancer. (Cancer Lett). Patient-derived malignant-effusion organoids (48 from 58 samples) used to guide therapy and study 3rd-generation EGFR-TKI resistance; translational model with clinical angle. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740831/
- Thoracic Radiotherapy and Radiation Time Would Affect the Efficacy of Advanced Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer for Patients Who Responded to TKIs Treatment. (Clin Lung Cancer). Retrospective cohort of 815 EGFR-mutant patients comparing thoracic radiotherapy before versus after thoracic progression on EGFR TKIs; non-randomised. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740297/
- Efficacy and safety of aumolertinib/icotinib combination therapy for naive EGFR-mutant non-small-cell lung cancer patients with brain metastases: A phase I/II study. (Neuro Oncol). Phase I/II trial of aumolertinib plus icotinib in 24 treatment-naive EGFR-mutant patients with brain metastases (median PFS 21.1 months, iORR 91.7%, grade >=3 TRAEs 37.5%); CNS data but small, single-arm. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41729955/
- Efficacy and safety of second-line treatments in patients with advanced EGFR-mutated non-small cell lung cancer progressing on osimertinib without identified targetable resistance mechanism at progression. (Respir Med Res). French multicentre retrospective study of 72 patients describing second-line outcomes after osimertinib without a targetable resistance mechanism (chemo rwPFS 5.8 months); post-osimertinib real-world data but small. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41722475/
- Efficacy of immune checkpoint inhibitors in the first line therapy of non-squamous non-small cell lung cancer: A systematic review and network meta-analysis. (Crit Rev Oncol Hematol). Bayesian network meta-analysis of 15 phase III RCTs of first-line PD-(L)1 regimens in non-squamous NSCLC without EGFR/ALK alterations; rigorous meta-analysis of RCTs. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41720325/
- Stabilin-1+ lipid-associated macrophages promote lung adenocarcinoma liver metastasis and osimertinib resistance through impairing macrophage phagocytosis via SIRPα-CD47 axis. (Drug Resist Updat). scRNA-seq and functional study implicating Stabilin-1+ lipid-associated macrophages in liver metastasis and osimertinib resistance via SIRPalpha-CD47; mechanistic with a possible therapeutic angle. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41719890/
- Sacituzumab tirumotecan improved survival for patients with EGFR TKI-resistant, EGFR-mutant advanced NSCLC. (Cancer). News item reporting that sacituzumab tirumotecan improved survival in EGFR TKI-resistant EGFR-mutant NSCLC; abstract missing and secondary news coverage rather than the primary trial report. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41712429/
- FIN-EGFRprint: a Finnish real-world study on treatments and outcomes in advanced NSCLC with common EGFR mutations. (Acta Oncol). Finnish real-world study of 379 patients with common EGFR mutations showing improved TTNT and OS across TKI eras (median OS 19.1 to 29.3 months); large real-world cohort. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41711491/
- Cutaneous Toxicities Associated With Amivantamab in Patients With Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: A Systematic Review. (Int J Dermatol). Systematic review of cutaneous toxicities of amivantamab in NSCLC; abstract missing, judged from title as relevant safety evidence for an EGFR bispecific. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41709424/
- Real-World Clinical Outcomes and Treatment Patterns in Advanced/Metastatic EGFR-Mutant NSCLC After Progression on First-Line Osimertinib for French Patients. (Adv Ther). French ESME real-world database study of 284 patients receiving second-line therapy after first-line osimertinib (median rwOS 10.1 months, rwPFS 4.1 months); large post-osimertinib real-world cohort. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41686417/
- Structural Studies of Fourth-Generation EGFR Inhibitors Reveal Insights into Selective T790M and C797S Targeting. (ACS Med Chem Lett). X-ray co-crystal structures of 4th-generation EGFR inhibitors BI-8128 and BI-4732 against T790M/C797S; preclinical work on 4th-generation TKIs. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41684669/
- Recurrent Escape from Osimertinib-Induced Senescence Promotes Genomic Instability Associated with Therapeutic Resistance. (bioRxiv). Preprint, not peer reviewed. Preprint (not peer reviewed) cell-line study showing repeated escape from osimertinib-induced senescence promotes genomic instability and resistance; mechanistic resistance finding. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41676694/
- Efficacy and safety findings of the EXTRA study in older adult EGFR-mutant lung cancer patients receiving afatinib as first-line treatment. (Sci Rep). Prospective EXTRA study analysis comparing afatinib dose-reduction efficacy and safety in older (n=32) versus younger (n=71) EGFR-mutant patients; small subgroups. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41673455/
- Amivantamab plus chemotherapy versus chemotherapy for first-line treatment of participants with EGFR exon 20 insertion-mutated advanced non-small cell lung cancer: PAPILLON Asia subgroup analysis. (Lung Cancer). Asian subgroup (n=186) of phase III PAPILLON confirming PFS benefit of amivantamab-chemotherapy (HR 0.34); consistent with the main result but a subgroup. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41671628/
- Real-world occurrence of severe gastrointestinal bleeding in patients receiving amivantamab plus lazertinib: A two-case series among 25 consecutive cases. (Lung Cancer). Single-centre series reporting life-threatening GI bleeding in 2 of 25 consecutive patients (8%) on amivantamab plus lazertinib; small but a potential new safety signal for a first-line EGFR regimen. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41671627/
- Radiotherapy combined with third-generation EGFR tyrosine kinase inhibitor in first-line treatment of advanced oligometastatic non-small cell lung cancer: a single-center, retrospective study. (J Thorac Dis). Single-centre retrospective cohort (n=260) of radiotherapy plus 3rd-generation EGFR TKI in oligometastatic disease; no overall PFS/OS benefit, PFS gain only with BED10 >=50 Gy subgroup. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41660475/
- Prognostic factors of epidermal growth factor receptor (EGFR)-mutated advanced non-small cell lung cancer treated with EGFR-tyrosine kinase inhibitor (TKI): a nationwide registry study in China. (Transl Lung Cancer Res). Nationwide Chinese registry of 221,788 EGFR-TKI-treated advanced NSCLC patients (83,791 in survival analysis; median OS 3.2 years) identifying prognostic factors; very large real-world cohort. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41659261/
- Clinical implications of plasma EGFR T790M and ctDNA shedding across metastatic sites in plasma- or tissue-confirmed EGFR-mutant non-small cell lung cancer treated with lazertinib: a prospective multicenter cohort study. (Transl Lung Cancer Res). Prospective multicentre cohort of 117 lazertinib-treated patients showing plasma T790M positivity associated with shorter PFS (10.0 vs 23.0 months) and OS; ctDNA prognostic data. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41659259/
- Interpretable deep learning model of circulating genomics for quantitative survival prediction in advanced non-small cell lung cancer. (Clin Transl Oncol). Deep-learning ctDNA survival model built on 1,373 advanced NSCLC patients from two ultra-deep ctDNA assays; large dataset with validation. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41649698/
- Prevalence and Actionability of MTAP Loss in Oncogene-Driven Lung Cancer. (bioRxiv). Preprint, not peer reviewed. Preprint (not peer reviewed): MTAP loss in 17% of 240 NSCLC patients (24% of stage IV driver-positive) and PRMT5 inhibitor plus TKI activity in cell lines, including EGFR-mutant ones. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41648424/
- Prolonged progression-free survival on epidermal growth factor receptor-tyrosine kinase inhibitors predicts superior response to atezolizumab-based therapy over chemotherapy in epidermal growth factor receptor-mutated non-small-cell lung cancer following progression. (ESMO Real World Data Digit Oncol). Multicentre retrospective study (n=350) showing longer prior EGFR-TKI benefit predicts better PFS with atezolizumab-bevacizumab-chemotherapy than chemotherapy after TKI progression (8.6 vs 5.3 months after matching). https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41647697/
- Interlesional Heterogeneity of EGFR Mutations: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis. (Mol Diagn Ther). Systematic review and meta-analysis of 154 studies (15,560 patients) finding 16.1% pooled EGFR mutation discordance across lesions/biopsies, higher after TKI exposure; informative for testing strategy. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41642450/
- Cost-effectiveness analysis of rezivertinib and gefitinib in patients with EGFR-mutated advanced non-small cell lung cancer. (Expert Rev Pharmacoecon Outcomes Res). Cost-effectiveness Markov model of first-line rezivertinib versus gefitinib in China based on the REZOR trial; relevant to access. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41630689/
- Discovery of Highly Selective AKR1C3 Inhibitors to Overcome EGFR C797S-Mediated Osimertinib Resistance in Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer. (J Med Chem). Medicinal chemistry and xenograft study of AKR1C3 inhibitor SG-55 synergising with osimertinib against C797S/T790M resistance; preclinical with in vivo data. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41612551/
- Treatment adherence and clinical outcomes of osimertinib in minority patients with advanced EGFR mutated NSCLC. (NPJ Precis Oncol). Single-centre US retrospective cohort (n=174) showing worse OS for non-Hispanic Black versus White patients on osimertinib despite similar adherence; relevant to outcome disparities. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41593317/
- A combined strategy of EGFR-MET bispecific antibody and HER3 ADC to overcome osimertinib resistance in NSCLC. (Cell Oncol (Dordr)). Preclinical in vitro and in vivo study combining amivantamab with patritumab deruxtecan in osimertinib-resistant models; credible path to the clinic for post-osimertinib combinations. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41591580/
- The Landscape of CEACAM5 Expression by Immunohistochemistry in NSCLC. (JTO Clin Res Rep). IHC landscape of CEACAM5 expression (an ADC target) across two real-world NSCLC cohorts with prognostic analysis; relevant to ADC development. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41584721/
- Bevacizumab Plus EGFR-TKIs vs EGFR-TKIs Alone for Advanced EGFR-Mutant NSCLC: A Meta-Analysis. (Clin Med Insights Oncol). Meta-analysis of 14 RCTs showing bevacizumab plus EGFR TKIs improves ORR and 1-year PFS/OS versus TKI alone in EGFR-mutant NSCLC; meta-analysis of RCTs, with older-generation TKIs. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41584313/
- Efficacy and safety of SMET12 in combination with toripalimab and chemotherapy in advanced non-small-cell lung cancer patients tested positive for EGFR protein who are treatment-naïve or harbor acquired resistance to standard therapy: a phase 2, multi-cohort clinical trial. (Front Immunol). Multi-cohort phase 2 trial (n=32) of the EGFR x CD3 bispecific SMET12 with toripalimab and chemotherapy, including a TKI-resistant EGFR-mutant cohort (ORR 41.7%, median PFS 7.2 months); new agent but very small cohorts. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41583476/
- EGFR-mutant transformed small cell lung cancer harbors intratumoral heterogeneity targetable with MEK inhibitor combination therapy.
